# St. Claire — Population & Characters

The single searchable record of every named person in St. Claire, regardless of role, status, or how minor. This is the duplicate-checking backbone for the whole project — before naming anyone new, check here first.

**Source of truth order:** St_Claire_00_Concept.md governs the lore. St_Claire_00B_Rules_and_Mechanics.md governs the checkable rules and generation process. St_Claire_01_Founding_Pioneers.md holds founding-era history for the twelve. This document holds everyone's current status — including the twelve founding mothers, per the two-record rule, even when their entry here is minimal (e.g. "retired — journalist" or "deceased").

**Related documents (the full schema):** St_Claire_04_Households.md, St_Claire_05_Organizations.md, St_Claire_06_Places.md, St_Claire_07_Occupations.md. Character records here cross-reference all four rather than duplicating their data.

**Population process:** full rule in St_Claire_00B_Rules_and_Mechanics.md. Names are added here in small batches, checked against everyone already listed before adding more.

## Character Record Template

Every entry in this document uses this format:

- **Name**
- **Status:** active / retired / deceased
- **Birthdate**
- **Tags** — any category memberships this person holds, as a list (e.g. "Founding Mother — leadership & governance," "Informal Mayor," "Council member"). A person can carry multiple tags; this is how categorization works instead of splitting the document into separate sections.
- **Physical description & presentation** — how she looks, how she presents (per Who St. Claire Is For and Feminism & Gender Expression) — enough to actually picture her, not just a label
- **Voice** — how she talks, a real personality signature
- **Occupation** — from the Occupation lookup list (St_Claire_07)
- **Role/Title at Organization** — if applicable; references a specific Organization (St_Claire_05) and whatever title it uses, unless that org's own record flags an exception. Informal, non-Organization-based social roles (like the Mayor) belong in Tags, not here.
- **Routine/regular haunts** — where this character habitually is outside of work/home: a standing diner order, a bar she's a regular at, a weekly walk — specific enough to answer "would she plausibly be here" for a given day/time, not just a vague hobby list
- **Household** — references a Household record (St_Claire_04) rather than duplicating residence/relationship data
- **Arrival story** — how/when she joined the community (per the Rites of Passage intake process), or "born here"
- **Notable milestones** — Bloom Day, wedding, etc., if relevant
- **One real, specific trait** — something that makes her a person, not a list of facts
- **Connections** — mentor/mentee, family, named relationships to others already in this document

## Child Record Template

Children get a deliberately different, minimal template — not the adult presentation/voice fields above, which are specifically about adult lesbian identity culture and don't apply to children (per Concept.md, Family Formation & Co-Parenting):

- **Name**
- **Age**
- **Household** — references a Household record (St_Claire_04)
- **School grade** — if applicable
- **Personality/interests** — brief, real specifics, not a full adult-style profile

---

## Population

**Ruth Halloway**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: June 14, 1952
- Tags: Founding Mother — leadership & governance; Informal Mayor
- Physical description & presentation: Butch. White. Average height (5'4"–5'8"). Average build. Short, dark hair, salt-and-pepper, a denim jacket covered in old pins.
- Voice: Dry, understated humor.
- Occupation: Director/President of the St. Claire Foundation (the nonprofit running the community's safety net — see St_Claire_00_Concept.md, Safety Net & Mutual Aid). This role is what earned the community's trust that led to her also holding the Mayor position — the two aren't unrelated facts about her.
- Role/Title at Organization: Director/President, St. Claire Foundation (cross-reference: Organization record not yet created in St_Claire_05)
- Household: Partnered with Diane Merchant since May 20, 1989 (met April 11, 1987, wedding September 7, 1991) — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: led the 1974 squat itself, beginning March 3, 1974 — present at St. Claire's founding, not an arrival in the usual sense
- Notable milestones: came out in her late teens, before arriving in St. Claire (predates the community); no Bloom Day (cis lesbian)
- One real, specific trait: keeps a hand-rolled cigarette perpetually unlit behind her ear — quit decades ago, never lost the habit of having one there
- Connections: partnered with Diane Merchant; founding-generation peer of the Historian (same 1974 movement, distinct roles)

**Diane Merchant**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: March 3, 1958
- Tags: none
- Physical description & presentation: High Femme. White. Average height. Fuller figure. Silver hair worn long — a real style choice carried from earlier decades of dyeing, not just an aging default, a soft cardigan over a sundress, a sculpted midi dress with a dramatic feminine silhouette, polished jewelry, a defined lip, and 4-inch stilettos.
- Voice: Warm, direct — a natural counterbalance to Ruth's dry understatement.
- Occupation: Owner, Merchant's Blooms (florist)
- Role/Title at Organization: Owner, Merchant's Blooms (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Household: Partnered with Ruth Halloway since May 20, 1989 (met April 11, 1987, wedding September 7, 1991) — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: arrived in St. Claire March 15, 1982, drawn to the community already forming; not born here
- Notable milestones: wedding with Ruth, September 7, 1991
- One real, specific trait: genuinely cannot walk past a wilting plant anywhere in the district without stopping to fix it, whether it's hers or not
- Connections: partnered with Ruth Halloway

**Juliette "Jules" Marchand**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: August 22, 1988
- Tags: none
- Physical description & presentation: Femme, high-energy presence. White. Average height, wiry build. Tattoos up both forearms. Almost always in a crisp fitted white chef's coat, sleeves pushed up, with a knee-length A-line skirt and non-slip 3.5-inch heeled work boots; short sandy-blonde hair.
- Voice: Fast-talking, blunt, genuinely passionate the moment food comes up — no patience for small talk otherwise.
- Occupation: Head chef, The River Mill Eatery
- Role/Title at Organization: Head Chef, The River Mill Eatery (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: at the restaurant most nights it's open; known to walk the Saturday market at Keystone Lofts early, before her shift, picking produce herself; Blush on Friday and Saturday nights
- Household: single (not seeking) — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: not yet determined
- Notable milestones: not yet determined
- One real, specific trait: farm-to-table isn't a menu description to her, it's close to a moral position — she'll actually argue about it if pushed
- Connections: none yet established

**Betty "Ma" Higgins**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: April 2, 1958
- Tags: Founding Mother — commerce
- Physical description & presentation: Butch, weathered. White. Average height, sturdy build. Gray hair cropped short. Decades of early mornings visible in her hands and face, a worn leather jacket, sleeves pushed up.
- Voice: booming laugh that carries to the parking lot; calls everyone "honey" without exception
- Occupation: Owner, The Sunrise Counter
- Role/Title at Organization: Owner, The Sunrise Counter (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: at the counter most mornings — four decades running
- Household: partnered with Dot Jenkins
- Arrival story: took over her mother's diner counter routine as a teenager, working shifts before school; by twenty she was running it outright and never left
- Notable milestones: has fed every mayor, informal or otherwise, since Ruth Halloway's earliest days; the counter has genuinely never closed for a single business day in four decades, storms included
- One real, specific trait: the institutional anchor of the district's morning — the diner runs on her presence
- Connections: partnered with Dot Jenkins

**Teodora "Teo" Alcalá**
- Status: deceased (2019)
- Birthdate: June 19, 1949
- Tags: Founding figure — urban planning & landscape architecture
- Physical description & presentation: Butch. Latina (Mexican American). Tall, weathered hands, always sketching on whatever paper was nearby, even decades later, short dark hair, slicked back.
- Voice: precise about her craft, stubborn about her vision, warm about everything else
- Occupation: trained landscape architect (formal city-planning credentials, rare for the squat generation); self-taught in radical feminist planning theory
- Role/Title at Organization: none — worked independently and through the St. Claire Foundation, never took a formal city title
- Routine/regular haunts: (historical) walked the working riverfront and the parish grounds daily for years, sketchbook in hand
- Household: partnered, decades, to a woman never fully documented in the record — flag if this should be built out
- Arrival story: joined the 1974 squat within its first year, recognized immediately by Ruth Halloway as someone whose formal training could be turned toward the community's own values rather than the city's
- Notable milestones: authored the district's original hand-drawn "womb plan" in 1976, two years after the squat began; fought for and lost the 1988 zoning battle over Downtown's street layout (see St_Claire_00_Concept.md); lived to see Halloway Park's Hollow trail network completed in 2003
- One real, specific trait: the actual designer of the Connective Tissue trail network — not a metaphor invented after the fact, a real person's real drafting-table work, later formalized into the district's urban form
- Connections: worked closely with Ruth Halloway from the earliest days of the squat; mentored a generation of the district's later planners and landscape staff, including Elena Rostova

**Odessa Calloway**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: February 11, 1953
- Tags: Founding Mother — history & oral tradition
- Physical description & presentation: Butch. White. Average height, weathered hands, decades of work still visible in her bearing, close-cropped brown hair, practical cargo pants and a henley.
- Voice: unhurried, treats the past as something living and taught, not commemorated once a year and forgotten
- Occupation: Founder/Director, Odessa Calloway's Keeping House
- Role/Title at Organization: Founder/Director, Odessa Calloway's Keeping House (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: Odessa Calloway's Keeping House, most days even now
- Household: single — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: one of the working-class butches who took the riverfront factories in 1974, alongside Ruth Halloway
- Notable milestones: when the parish closed in the early 2000s, directed the squatters' generation's own resources toward preserving its physical legacy — the bridge between St. Claire's two founding histories, made concrete in one person's life
- Notable milestones: founded Odessa Calloway's Keeping House specifically so the community's oral history had a real, living home rather than scattered boxes
- One real, specific trait: captures the community's oral history directly, in person, rather than letting it calcify into official record
- Connections: founding-generation peer of Ruth Halloway and Josefina Alvarado

**Wren Iverson**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: November 3, 1994
- Tags: none
- Physical description & presentation: Femme. White. Average height, always seems to be in the middle of reading something, short dark hair, deliberately styled, a soft flannel, sleeves rolled to the elbow, a feminine knit top with a pleated midi skirt and 3.75-inch block heels.
- Voice: quiet, genuinely reverent about the material she lives alongside
- Occupation: Live-in caretaker, Odessa Calloway's Keeping House
- Role/Title at Organization: Live-in Caretaker, Odessa Calloway's Keeping House (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: Odessa Calloway's Keeping House — literally lives there
- Household: single — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: grew up in Residential Infill, volunteered at the Keeping House as a teenager cataloguing donated photographs, and simply never stopped; Odessa offered her the live-in caretaker role the year she turned twenty-two
- Notable milestones: has personally recorded over sixty oral history interviews with longtime residents, several with founding mothers themselves before any of their formal markers existed in the Grove
- One real, specific trait: the generational bridge — young enough to represent the community's future, living daily alongside its entire physical past
- Connections: colleague of Odessa Calloway

**Marion Castellano**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: October 4, 1957
- Tags: Founding Mother — journalism & press
- Physical description & presentation: Trans woman. Femme. Italian American. Average height, sharp eyes, decades of newsroom energy still visible, curly red hair often pinned back, a belted shirt-dress with a full skirt and 4-inch heels.
- Voice: direct, demanding of the truth, genuinely warm to anyone she's mentoring
- Occupation: Founder/Editor-in-Chief, Marion Castellano's St. Claire Beacon
- Role/Title at Organization: Founder/Editor-in-Chief, Marion Castellano's St. Claire Beacon (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: the Beacon's newsroom, most days even now, semi-retired
- Household: single — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: arrived early in the community's growth, recognized St. Claire needed a real press with real teeth, not just a newsletter
- Notable milestones: built the Beacon into an outlet with real, exceptional recognition in journalism at large — multiple Pulitzers and Emmys, not just local acclaim
- One real, specific trait: editorial mission was always demystify, not just celebrate — show that lesbians and trans people are real, authentic human beings, not the alien "other" outside perception often makes them
- Connections: built mentorship directly into the newsroom's culture, training a generation of the district's journalists to find and use their own voice

**Dr. Harriet Osei**
- Status: active
- Birthdate: January 15, 1957
- Tags: Founding figure — Collective Utility System
- Physical description & presentation: Butch. Black. Average height, practical, engineer's bearing.
- Voice: precise, genuinely excited by real infrastructure problems, no patience for wasted resources
- Occupation: environmental/civil engineer; Founder, Harriet Osei – St. Claire Reclamation Works
- Role/Title at Organization: Founder/Director, Harriet Osei – St. Claire Reclamation Works (cross-reference: St_Claire_05_Organizations.md)
- Routine/regular haunts: the central processing facility, most days even now
- Household: single — see Household record in St_Claire_04_Households.md
- Arrival story: encountered the real technology directly as a young engineer during Disney's actual promotional window for it — Magic Kingdom's AVAC system opened in 1971, and Disney spent the 1970s actively showcasing it to planners, engineers, and press as a model for "communities of the future" (a real historical push that never caught on beyond Disney and Roosevelt Island). Osei arrived in St. Claire in the early 1980s already carrying the vision, but the community wasn't secure enough to attempt something this ambitious until after the 1988 land-trust win — she waited years to actually build what she'd already designed.
- Notable milestones: studied real precedents directly — Disney's AVAC system, New York's steam network, Milwaukee's Milorganite program — and saw that St. Claire could combine and improve on all three rather than picking one; built the regional tipping-fee business model that turned the whole system from self-sufficient into genuinely profitable
- One real, specific trait: the reason St. Claire residents don't pay a utility bill — not an abstract policy, one engineer's actual business model, still running
- Connections: mentored several of the district's later civil/environmental engineers; her work is a direct, real precedent for St. Claire Builders & Co.'s current infrastructure responsibilities
